========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, my wife made the appointment. She's been... she's been really worried lately. We've been married for twenty-two years, you know? Met at the community college back when I was getting my associate's degree. She was studying accounting, always so good with numbers. I was never good with numbers, always preferred working with my hands. But anyway, she's the one who wanted me to come here. What finally scared her is that I keep hearing things. Like, I hear water running, and these men whispering inside the walls. I tell her about it, but nobody's there when she checks. Our house is old, built in the fifties, got those plaster walls that are a nightmare to fix if you ever have to put a hole in them. I tried to hang a picture once and the whole section just crumbled. But it's not just the noise. Sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I'll go over and touch it, and it's completely dry. It doesn't make any sense. My wife's uncle had a plumbing leak that ruined his entire basement, mold everywhere, cost him thousands. I just... I don't know what's happening.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. 

But anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.

Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. It's more just... I know what's going on. I already mentioned it, but I know it sounds crazy, I really do. I don't know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything else that others might not notice, like shadows moving when no one is there or voices that aren't coming from any person nearby?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. \n\nBut anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. The lady across the street, she has this little yappy dog, a Pomeranian or something. My aunt had a Pomeranian when I was a kid, mean little thing, bit my ankle right through my sock once. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, exactly. Like I was just trying to explain... they are definitely watching me. I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain that the mold and the old wiring in the house have poisoned everything I own. It's ruined it all. And they know it. So they're monitoring me, just waiting for me to finally mess up. 

Monitoring... you know, speaking of monitoring, my brother-in-law put up one of those doorbell cameras last Thanksgiving. The ones that send a video right to your phone? He caught a raccoon trying to get into his garbage cans at two in the morning. Raccoons are incredibly smart, they have those little hands that can open latches. We had one get into our camping cooler back in '98, ate all the hot dogs but left the buns. My wife was so mad, she had spent all morning packing that cooler with ice. We had to drive twenty miles to a gas station just to get something for dinner... 

But... what was I saying? Right. The neighbors. They don't need doorbell cameras to monitor me. They just use their sprinklers and their dogs. They're absolutely targeting me, just watching and waiting for the poison to finally take me down.

But it's not just the noise. Sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I'll go over and touch it, and it's completely dry. It doesn't make any sense. My wife's uncle had a plumbing leak that ruined his entire basement, mold everywhere, cost him thousands. I just... I don't know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything else that others might not notice, like shadows moving when no one is there or voices that aren't coming from any person nearby?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. \n\nBut anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? Like things people say or do are directed at you specifically, even if they don't seem to be?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the old wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's just a fact. And the neighbors... they don't have to say anything to me. I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. The lady across the street, she has this little yappy dog, a Pomeranian or something. My aunt had a Pomeranian when I was a kid, mean little thing, bit my ankle right through my sock once. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. That's how you know they're watching, right? When they go out of their way to pretend they aren't. They probably all talk about me at the neighborhood block party. We used to go to those, back when my wife would make her famous potato salad. She puts a little bit of dill in it, really changes the whole flavor profile. But I haven't had an appetite for potato salad, or anything really, in months. Everything just tastes like... dust. Poisoned dust.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people's actions — like the neighbor adjusting his sprinklers or the woman walking her dog — are specifically targeted at you or meant to harm or monitor you in some way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, exactly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. 

Watching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. 

But... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. 

Wiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. 

But... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.

I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. \n\nBut anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? Like things people say or do are directed at you specifically, even if they don't seem to be?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. It's more just... I know what's going on. I already mentioned it, but I know it sounds crazy, I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the old wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's just a fact. And the neighbors... they don't have to say anything to me. I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. The lady across the street, she has this little yappy dog, a Pomeranian or something. My aunt had a Pomeranian when I was a kid, mean little thing, bit my ankle right through my sock once. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. That's how you know they're watching, right? When they go out of their way to pretend they aren't. They probably all talk about me at the neighborhood block party. We used to go to those, back when my wife would make her famous potato salad. She puts a little bit of dill in it, really changes the whole flavor profile. But I haven't had an appetite for potato salad, or anything really, in months. Everything just tastes like... dust. Poisoned dust.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people's actions — like the neighbor adjusting his sprinklers or the woman walking her dog — are specifically targeted at you or meant to harm or monitor you in some way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, exactly. Like I was just trying to explain... they are definitely watching me. I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain that the mold and the old wiring in the house have poisoned everything I own. It's ruined it all. And they know it. So they're monitoring me, just waiting for me to finally mess up. \n\nMonitoring... you know, speaking of monitoring, my brother-in-law put up one of those doorbell cameras last Thanksgiving. The ones that send a video right to your phone? He caught a raccoon trying to get into his garbage cans at two in the morning. Raccoons are incredibly smart, they have those little hands that can open latches. We had one get into our camping cooler back in '98, ate all the hot dogs but left the buns. My wife was so mad, she had spent all morning packing that cooler with ice. We had to drive twenty miles to a gas station just to get something for dinner... \n\nBut... what was I saying? Right. The neighbors. They don't need doorbell cameras to monitor me. They just use their sprinklers and their dogs. They're absolutely targeting me, just watching and waiting for the poison to finally take me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are trying to harm you or that they are plotting against you in any way beyond just watching you?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like other people can tell your belongings are poisoned, or do you think it's something only you can sense?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m certain the mold and wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They can tell. I mean, they have to be able to tell, right? That's why they're watching me. Like I keep saying—and I know, I know it sounds completely far-fetched, but I am just certain of it—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And the neighbors know it. They can tell, and they're just watching to see when I finally mess up again. 

Mess up... you know, speaking of messing up, I tried to fix a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom last spring. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip. Drove me absolutely crazy at night. I went to the hardware store and bought all the washers and a new wrench. The guy at the hardware store was wearing this bright orange apron. It reminded me of those safety vests they make highway workers wear. I always feel bad for those guys, working out in the blazing sun all summer with the asphalt just radiating heat. I remember driving through Nevada once, years ago, and the road looked like it was melting. We had to pull over at this little diner because the car was overheating. I ordered a slice of cherry pie, but the cherries were so tart I couldn't even finish it. My wife ended up eating it. She always loved sour things. Sour Patch Kids, lemon drops... she used to buy those giant bags of lemons and just eat them like oranges. I never understood how she didn't ruin the enamel on her teeth. The dentist always told her to be careful with that. 

But... what was the question? Oh, if other people can sense the poison. Yeah, the neighbors definitely know. That's why they're watching me.

me.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Just walking from the kitchen to the back room to look at the tools can wear me out, and resting on the couch doesn’t bring the energy back.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before the house problems dragged on and I fell into this depression, I never heard voices in the walls, saw wet patches that weren’t there, or thought the neighbors were tracking me. Those things only showed up after I had already stopped caring about my usual life and started feeling like a failure every day.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’ve stopped keeping up with basic repairs, bills, and commitments, and my wife has had to handle decisions I used to manage myself. Things got bad enough that she called a crisis team because I couldn’t cooperate with a safety inspection at the house.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since the winter freeze turned the house repairs into a mess I couldn’t get out from under.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are trying to harm you or that they are plotting against you in any way beyond just watching you?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like other people can tell your belongings are poisoned, or do you think it's something only you can sense?\n\nPatient: They can tell. I mean, they have to be able to tell, right? That's why they're watching me. Like I keep saying—and I know, I know it sounds completely far-fetched, but I am just certain of it—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And the neighbors know it. They can tell, and they're just watching to see when I finally mess up again. \n\nMess up... you know, speaking of messing up, I tried to fix a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom last spring. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip. Drove me absolutely crazy at night. I went to the hardware store and bought all the washers and a new wrench. The guy at the hardware store was wearing this bright orange apron. It reminded me of those safety vests they make highway workers wear. I always feel bad for those guys, working out in the blazing sun all summer with the asphalt just radiating heat. I remember driving through Nevada once, years ago, and the road looked like it was melting. We had to pull over at this little diner because the car was overheating. I ordered a slice of cherry pie, but the cherries were so tart I couldn't even finish it. My wife ended up eating it. She always loved sour things. Sour Patch Kids, lemon drops... she used to buy those giant bags of lemons and just eat them like oranges. I never understood how she didn't ruin the enamel on her teeth. The dentist always told her to be careful with that. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, if other people can sense the poison. Yeah, the neighbors definitely know. That's why they're watching me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you believe that other people, like friends or family members, also know that your belongings are poisoned, or do you think you're the only one who truly understands it?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They know. My family, I mean. I haven't seen them much lately, but they know. They have to. Like I've been saying, and I know how completely crazy and far-fetched it sounds, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, just like the neighbors know it. They're all just watching to see when I mess up again.

Family... my sister actually called me a couple of weeks ago. Or maybe it was a month ago? I can't keep track of time anymore. She lives out in Oregon now. Portland. It rains all the time out there. I went to visit her once, maybe ten years ago, and I forgot to pack an umbrella. I had to buy one at this little corner store near her apartment. It was one of those cheap, flimsy ones with a plastic handle shaped like a duck's head. I thought it was funny at first, but the beak kept catching on my coat sleeve. It was a nice coat, too—a gray wool peacoat I found at a thrift store for like twenty bucks. You don't find deals like that anymore. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! You can buy a brand new one at Target for twenty. And you don't even know if the used one works. Or what kind of bread they put in it. Some people eat that really dense pumpernickel bread. It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I used to try to eat it when I was trying to be healthy, but... well, like I said, I don't really have an appetite for anything these days anyway. 

But... yeah. My family knows. They know about the poison, and they know the neighbors are watching me. Everyone knows.

Our house is old, built in the fifties, got those plaster walls that are a nightmare to fix if you ever have to put a hole in them. I tried to hang a picture once and the whole section just crumbled. It was one of those cheap, flimsy ones with a plastic handle shaped like a duck's head. I thought it was funny at first, but the beak kept catching on my coat sleeve. It was a nice coat, too—a gray wool peacoat I found at a thrift store for like twenty bucks. You don't find deals like that anymore. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! You can buy a brand new one at Target for twenty. And you don't even know if the used one works. Or what kind of bread they put in it. Some people eat that really dense pumpernickel bread. It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I used to try to eat it when I was trying to be healthy, but... well, like I said, I don't really have an appetite for anything these days anyway. \n\nBut... yeah. My family knows. They know about the poison, and they know the neighbors are watching me. Everyone knows.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your family or the neighbors are working together in any way regarding the poisoning, or do you believe they each just know about it independently?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m certain the mold and wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Working together? I... I don't know if they talk to each other about it. But they both know. Like I keep telling you—and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am just absolutely certain—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. 

Watching... you know, my neighbor across the street, he has this massive German Shepherd. Beautiful dog, but it just sits in the front window all day long, staring out. Just watching the street. It barks at the mailman every single day, right at 11:30. You could set your watch by it. I used to have a watch, a nice Seiko with a leather band. My grandfather gave it to me when I graduated high school. It had this little dial that showed the phases of the moon. I always thought that was so fascinating, tracking the moon. I wanted to be an astronaut when I was a kid. Didn't everyone? I had this poster of the Apollo 11 moon landing on my bedroom wall, right next to a poster of a Lamborghini Countach. The white one. Man, those cars looked like spaceships themselves. My cousin actually got to sit in one once at a car show in Chicago. Chicago gets so cold in the winter, the wind just cuts right through you off the lake. I went there for a conference once, and I forgot my scarf. I had to buy this really itchy wool one at a kiosk in the train station. It made my neck break out in a rash. 

But... I'm sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, if they work together. I don't think they're coordinating or anything. They just all know about the poison, and they're all watching me.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my wife made the appointment. She's been... she's been really worried lately. We've been married for twenty-two years, you know? Met at the community college back when I was getting my associate's degree. She was studying accounting, always so good with numbers. I was never good with numbers, always preferred working with my hands. But anyway, she's the one who wanted me to come here. What finally scared her is that I keep hearing things. Like, I hear water running, and these men whispering inside the walls. I tell her about it, but nobody's there when she checks. Our house is old, built in the fifties, got those plaster walls that are a nightmare to fix if you ever have to put a hole in them. I tried to hang a picture once and the whole section just crumbled. My wife's uncle had a plumbing leak that ruined his entire basement, mold everywhere, cost him thousands. I just... I don't know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything else that others might not notice, like shadows moving when no one is there or voices that aren't coming from any person nearby?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? Like things people say or do are directed at you specifically, even if they don't seem to be?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. It's more just... I know what's going on. I already mentioned it, but I know it sounds crazy, I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the old wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's just a fact. And the neighbors... they don't have to say anything to me. I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. That's how you know they're watching, right? When they go out of their way to pretend they aren't. They probably all talk about me at the neighborhood block party. We used to go to those, back when my wife would make her famous potato salad. She puts a little bit of dill in it, really changes the whole flavor profile. But I haven't had an appetite for potato salad, or anything really, in months. Everything just tastes like... dust. Poisoned dust.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people's actions — like the neighbor adjusting his sprinklers or the woman walking her dog — are specifically targeted at you or meant to harm or monitor you in some way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, exactly. Like I was just trying to explain... they are definitely watching me. I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain that the mold and the old wiring in the house have poisoned everything I own. It's ruined it all. And they know it. So they're monitoring me, just waiting for me to finally mess up. \n\nMonitoring... you know, speaking of monitoring, my brother-in-law put up one of those doorbell cameras last Thanksgiving. The ones that send a video right to your phone? He caught a raccoon trying to get into his garbage cans at two in the morning. Raccoons are incredibly smart, they have those little hands that can open latches. We had one get into our camping cooler back in '98, ate all the hot dogs but left the buns. My wife was so mad, she had spent all morning packing that cooler with ice. We had to drive twenty miles to a gas station just to get something for dinner... \n\nBut... what was I saying? Right. The neighbors. They don't need doorbell cameras to monitor me. They just use their sprinklers and their dogs. They're absolutely targeting me, just watching and waiting for the poison to finally take me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are trying to harm you or that they are plotting against you in any way beyond just watching you?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like other people can tell your belongings are poisoned, or do you think it's something only you can sense?\n\nPatient: They can tell. I mean, they have to be able to tell, right? That's why they're watching me. Like I keep saying—and I know, I know it sounds completely far-fetched, but I am just certain of it—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And the neighbors know it. They can tell, and they're just watching to see when I finally mess up again. \n\nMess up... you know, speaking of messing up, I tried to fix a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom last spring. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip. Drove me absolutely crazy at night. I went to the hardware store and bought all the washers and a new wrench. The guy at the hardware store was wearing this bright orange apron. It reminded me of those safety vests they make highway workers wear. I always feel bad for those guys, working out in the blazing sun all summer with the asphalt just radiating heat. I remember driving through Nevada once, years ago, and the road looked like it was melting. We had to pull over at this little diner because the car was overheating. I ordered a slice of cherry pie, but the cherries were so tart I couldn't even finish it. My wife ended up eating it. She always loved sour things. Sour Patch Kids, lemon drops... she used to buy those giant bags of lemons and just eat them like oranges. I never understood how she didn't ruin the enamel on her teeth. The dentist always told her to be careful with that. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, if other people can sense the poison. Yeah, the neighbors definitely know. That's why they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that other people, like friends or family members, also know that your belongings are poisoned, or do you think you're the only one who truly understands it?\n\nPatient: They know. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! You can buy a brand new one at Target for twenty. And you don't even know if the used one works. Or what kind of bread they put in it. Some people eat that really dense pumpernickel bread. It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I used to try to eat it when I was trying to be healthy, but... well, like I said, I don't really have an appetite for anything these days anyway. \n\nBut... yeah. My family knows. They know about the poison, and they know the neighbors are watching me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I don't know if it's because of something I did. I just... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. 

Mess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. 

But... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.

Met at the community college back when I was getting my associate's degree. She was studying accounting, always so good with numbers. I was never good with numbers, always preferred working with my hands. But anyway, she's the one who wanted me to come here. What finally scared her is that I keep hearing things. Like, I hear water running, and these men whispering inside the walls. I tell her about it, but nobody's there when she checks. Our house is old, built in the fifties, got those plaster walls that are a nightmare to fix if you ever have to put a hole in them. I tried to hang a picture once and the whole section just crumbled. But it's not just the noise. Sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I'll go over and touch it, and it's completely dry. It doesn't make any sense. My wife's uncle had a plumbing leak that ruined his entire basement, mold everywhere, cost him thousands. I just... I don't know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything else that others might not notice, like shadows moving when no one is there or voices that aren't coming from any person nearby?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. \n\nBut anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? Like things people say or do are directed at you specifically, even if they don't seem to be?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. It's more just... I know what's going on. I already mentioned it, but I know it sounds crazy, I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the old wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's just a fact. And the neighbors... they don't have to say anything to me. I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. The lady across the street, she has this little yappy dog, a Pomeranian or something. My aunt had a Pomeranian when I was a kid, mean little thing, bit my ankle right through my sock once. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. That's how you know they're watching, right? When they go out of their way to pretend they aren't. They probably all talk about me at the neighborhood block party. We used to go to those, back when my wife would make her famous potato salad. She puts a little bit of dill in it, really changes the whole flavor profile. But I haven't had an appetite for potato salad, or anything really, in months. Everything just tastes like... dust. Poisoned dust.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people's actions — like the neighbor adjusting his sprinklers or the woman walking her dog — are specifically targeted at you or meant to harm or monitor you in some way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, exactly. Like I was just trying to explain... they are definitely watching me. I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain that the mold and the old wiring in the house have poisoned everything I own. It's ruined it all. And they know it. So they're monitoring me, just waiting for me to finally mess up. \n\nMonitoring... you know, speaking of monitoring, my brother-in-law put up one of those doorbell cameras last Thanksgiving. The ones that send a video right to your phone? He caught a raccoon trying to get into his garbage cans at two in the morning. Raccoons are incredibly smart, they have those little hands that can open latches. We had one get into our camping cooler back in '98, ate all the hot dogs but left the buns. My wife was so mad, she had spent all morning packing that cooler with ice. We had to drive twenty miles to a gas station just to get something for dinner... \n\nBut... what was I saying? Right. The neighbors. They don't need doorbell cameras to monitor me. They just use their sprinklers and their dogs. They're absolutely targeting me, just watching and waiting for the poison to finally take me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are trying to harm you or that they are plotting against you in any way beyond just watching you?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like other people can tell your belongings are poisoned, or do you think it's something only you can sense?\n\nPatient: They can tell. I mean, they have to be able to tell, right? That's why they're watching me. Like I keep saying—and I know, I know it sounds completely far-fetched, but I am just certain of it—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And the neighbors know it. They can tell, and they're just watching to see when I finally mess up again. \n\nMess up... you know, speaking of messing up, I tried to fix a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom last spring. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip. Drove me absolutely crazy at night. I went to the hardware store and bought all the washers and a new wrench. The guy at the hardware store was wearing this bright orange apron. It reminded me of those safety vests they make highway workers wear. I always feel bad for those guys, working out in the blazing sun all summer with the asphalt just radiating heat. I remember driving through Nevada once, years ago, and the road looked like it was melting. We had to pull over at this little diner because the car was overheating. I ordered a slice of cherry pie, but the cherries were so tart I couldn't even finish it. My wife ended up eating it. She always loved sour things. Sour Patch Kids, lemon drops... she used to buy those giant bags of lemons and just eat them like oranges. I never understood how she didn't ruin the enamel on her teeth. The dentist always told her to be careful with that. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, if other people can sense the poison. Yeah, the neighbors definitely know. That's why they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that other people, like friends or family members, also know that your belongings are poisoned, or do you think you're the only one who truly understands it?\n\nPatient: They know. My family, I mean. I haven't seen them much lately, but they know. They have to. Like I've been saying, and I know how completely crazy and far-fetched it sounds, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, just like the neighbors know it. They're all just watching to see when I mess up again.\n\nFamily... my sister actually called me a couple of weeks ago. Or maybe it was a month ago? I can't keep track of time anymore. She lives out in Oregon now. Portland. It rains all the time out there. I went to visit her once, maybe ten years ago, and I forgot to pack an umbrella. I had to buy one at this little corner store near her apartment. It was one of those cheap, flimsy ones with a plastic handle shaped like a duck's head. I thought it was funny at first, but the beak kept catching on my coat sleeve. It was a nice coat, too—a gray wool peacoat I found at a thrift store for like twenty bucks. You don't find deals like that anymore. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! You can buy a brand new one at Target for twenty. And you don't even know if the used one works. Or what kind of bread they put in it. Some people eat that really dense pumpernickel bread. It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I used to try to eat it when I was trying to be healthy, but... well, like I said, I don't really have an appetite for anything these days anyway. \n\nBut... yeah. My family knows. They know about the poison, and they know the neighbors are watching me. Everyone knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family or the neighbors are working together in any way regarding the poisoning, or do you believe they each just know about it independently?\n\nPatient: Working together? I... I don't know if they talk to each other about it. But they both know. Like I keep telling you—and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am just absolutely certain—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, my neighbor across the street, he has this massive German Shepherd. Beautiful dog, but it just sits in the front window all day long, staring out. Just watching the street. It barks at the mailman every single day, right at 11:30. You could set your watch by it. I used to have a watch, a nice Seiko with a leather band. My grandfather gave it to me when I graduated high school. It had this little dial that showed the phases of the moon. I always thought that was so fascinating, tracking the moon. I wanted to be an astronaut when I was a kid. Didn't everyone? I had this poster of the Apollo 11 moon landing on my bedroom wall, right next to a poster of a Lamborghini Countach. The white one. Man, those cars looked like spaceships themselves. My cousin actually got to sit in one once at a car show in Chicago. Chicago gets so cold in the winter, the wind just cuts right through you off the lake. I went there for a conference once, and I forgot my scarf. I had to buy this really itchy wool one at a kiosk in the train station. It made my neck break out in a rash. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, if they work together. I don't think they're coordinating or anything. They just all know about the poison, and they're all watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are watching you because of something specific you've done, or do you believe they're watching you for reasons you can't control?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's because of something I did. I just... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before the house problems dragged on and I fell into this depression, I never heard voices in the walls, saw wet patches that weren’t there, or thought the neighbors were tracking me. Those things only showed up after I had already stopped caring about my usual life and started feeling like a failure every day.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’ve stopped keeping up with basic repairs, bills, and commitments, and my wife has had to handle decisions I used to manage myself. Things got bad enough that she called a crisis team because I couldn’t cooperate with a safety inspection at the house.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since the winter freeze turned the house repairs into a mess I couldn’t get out from under.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe that other people, like friends or family members, also know that your belongings are poisoned, or do you think you're the only one who truly understands it?\n\nPatient: They know. My family, I mean. I haven't seen them much lately, but they know. They have to. Like I've been saying, and I know how completely crazy and far-fetched it sounds, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, just like the neighbors know it. They're all just watching to see when I mess up again.\n\nFamily... my sister actually called me a couple of weeks ago. Or maybe it was a month ago? I can't keep track of time anymore. She lives out in Oregon now. Portland. It rains all the time out there. I went to visit her once, maybe ten years ago, and I forgot to pack an umbrella. I had to buy one at this little corner store near her apartment. It was one of those cheap, flimsy ones with a plastic handle shaped like a duck's head. I thought it was funny at first, but the beak kept catching on my coat sleeve. It was a nice coat, too—a gray wool peacoat I found at a thrift store for like twenty bucks. You don't find deals like that anymore. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I... I don't know if they talk to each other about it. But they both know. Like I keep telling you—and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am just absolutely certain—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, my neighbor across the street, he has this massive German Shepherd. Beautiful dog, but it just sits in the front window all day long, staring out. Just watching the street. It barks at the mailman every single day, right at 11:30. You could set your watch by it. I used to have a watch, a nice Seiko with a leather band. My grandfather gave it to me when I graduated high school. It had this little dial that showed the phases of the moon. I always thought that was so fascinating, tracking the moon. I wanted to be an astronaut when I was a kid. Didn't everyone? I had this poster of the Apollo 11 moon landing on my bedroom wall, right next to a poster of a Lamborghini Countach. The white one. Man, those cars looked like spaceships themselves. My cousin actually got to sit in one once at a car show in Chicago. Chicago gets so cold in the winter, the wind just cuts right through you off the lake. I went there for a conference once, and I forgot my scarf. I had to buy this really itchy wool one at a kiosk in the train station. It made my neck break out in a rash. \n\nBut... And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Harm me? I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. It's like... well, I know it sounds far-fetched, I keep saying that, but I'm just certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. 

Mess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. I tried rolling up a towel and stuffing it under the door, but it didn't help much. 

Anyway... I don't know about ill will. I just know they're watching me. Waiting for me to slip up with the poisoned stuff.
