Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my wife made the appointment. She's been... she's been really worried lately. We've been married for twenty-two years, you know? Met at the community college back when I was getting my associate's degree. She was studying accounting, always so good with numbers. I was never good with numbers, always preferred working with my hands. But anyway, she's the one who wanted me to come here. What finally scared her is that I keep hearing things. Like, I hear water running, and these men whispering inside the walls. I tell her about it, but nobody's there when she checks. Our house is old, built in the fifties, got those plaster walls that are a nightmare to fix if you ever have to put a hole in them. I tried to hang a picture once and the whole section just crumbled. But it's not just the noise. Sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I'll go over and touch it, and it's completely dry. It doesn't make any sense. My wife's uncle had a plumbing leak that ruined his entire basement, mold everywhere, cost him thousands. I just... I don't know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything else that others might not notice, like shadows moving when no one is there or voices that aren't coming from any person nearby?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. \n\nBut anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? Like things people say or do are directed at you specifically, even if they don't seem to be?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. It's more just... I know what's going on. I already mentioned it, but I know it sounds crazy, I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the old wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's just a fact. And the neighbors... they don't have to say anything to me. I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. The lady across the street, she has this little yappy dog, a Pomeranian or something. My aunt had a Pomeranian when I was a kid, mean little thing, bit my ankle right through my sock once. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. That's how you know they're watching, right? When they go out of their way to pretend they aren't. They probably all talk about me at the neighborhood block party. We used to go to those, back when my wife would make her famous potato salad. She puts a little bit of dill in it, really changes the whole flavor profile. But I haven't had an appetite for potato salad, or anything really, in months. Everything just tastes like... dust. Poisoned dust.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people's actions — like the neighbor adjusting his sprinklers or the woman walking her dog — are specifically targeted at you or meant to harm or monitor you in some way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, exactly. Like I was just trying to explain... they are definitely watching me. I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain that the mold and the old wiring in the house have poisoned everything I own. It's ruined it all. And they know it. So they're monitoring me, just waiting for me to finally mess up. \n\nMonitoring... you know, speaking of monitoring, my brother-in-law put up one of those doorbell cameras last Thanksgiving. The ones that send a video right to your phone? He caught a raccoon trying to get into his garbage cans at two in the morning. Raccoons are incredibly smart, they have those little hands that can open latches. We had one get into our camping cooler back in '98, ate all the hot dogs but left the buns. My wife was so mad, she had spent all morning packing that cooler with ice. We had to drive twenty miles to a gas station just to get something for dinner... \n\nBut... what was I saying? Right. The neighbors. They don't need doorbell cameras to monitor me. They just use their sprinklers and their dogs. They're absolutely targeting me, just watching and waiting for the poison to finally take me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are trying to harm you or that they are plotting against you in any way beyond just watching you?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. The guy at the hardware store was wearing this bright orange apron. It reminded me of those safety vests they make highway workers wear. I always feel bad for those guys, working out in the blazing sun all summer with the asphalt just radiating heat. I remember driving through Nevada once, years ago, and the road looked like it was melting. We had to pull over at this little diner because the car was overheating. I ordered a slice of cherry pie, but the cherries were so tart I couldn't even finish it. My wife ended up eating it. She always loved sour things. Sour Patch Kids, lemon drops... she used to buy those giant bags of lemons and just eat them like oranges. I never understood how she didn't ruin the enamel on her teeth. It was a nice coat, too—a gray wool peacoat I found at a thrift store for like twenty bucks. You don't find deals like that anymore. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! You can buy a brand new one at Target for twenty. And you don't even know if the used one works. Or what kind of bread they put in it. Some people eat that really dense pumpernickel bread. It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I used to try to eat it when I was trying to be healthy, but... well, like I said, I don't really have an appetite for anything these days anyway. \n\nBut... yeah. My family knows. They know about the poison, and they know the neighbors are watching me. Everyone knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family or the neighbors are working together in any way regarding the poisoning, or do you believe they each just know about it independently?\n\nPatient: Working together? Like I keep telling you—and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am just absolutely certain—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, my neighbor across the street, he has this massive German Shepherd. Beautiful dog, but it just sits in the front window all day long, staring out. Just watching the street. It barks at the mailman every single day, right at 11:30. You could set your watch by it. I used to have a watch, a nice Seiko with a leather band. My grandfather gave it to me when I graduated high school. It had this little dial that showed the phases of the moon. I always thought that was so fascinating, tracking the moon. I wanted to be an astronaut when I was a kid. Didn't everyone? I had this poster of the Apollo 11 moon landing on my bedroom wall, right next to a poster of a Lamborghini Countach. The white one. Man, those cars looked like spaceships themselves. My cousin actually got to sit in one once at a car show in Chicago. Chicago gets so cold in the winter, the wind just cuts right through you off the lake. I went there for a conference once, and I forgot my scarf. I had to buy this really itchy wool one at a kiosk in the train station. It made my neck break out in a rash. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, if they work together. I don't think they're coordinating or anything. They just all know about the poison, and they're all watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are watching you because of something specific you've done, or do you believe they're watching you for reasons you can't control?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's because of something I did. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are watching you with the intent to harm you, or do you think they’re just observing without any direct ill will?\n\nPatient: Harm me? I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. My cousin actually got to sit in one once at a car show in Chicago. Chicago gets so cold in the winter, the wind just cuts right through you off the lake. I went there for a conference once, and I forgot my scarf. I had to buy this really itchy wool one at a kiosk in the train station. It made my neck break out in a rash. \n\nBut... I just... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are watching you with the intent to harm you, or do you think they’re just observing without any direct ill will?\n\nPatient: Harm me? I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. It's like... well, I know it sounds far-fetched, I keep saying that, but I'm just certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. I tried rolling up a towel and stuffing it under the door, but it didn't help much. \n\nAnyway... I don't know about ill will. I just know they're watching me. Thoughts not my own? No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. Nobody is putting ideas in my head. I mean, the only thing that gets put in my head are those stupid commercial jingles, you know? Earworms. There's this one for a local mattress store that just plays on a loop in my brain sometimes. 

I haven't bought a new mattress in... God, maybe fifteen years. It's an old coil spring one. They say you're supposed to flip them every six months, but it's so heavy. I tried flipping it by myself once and I knocked over my bedside lamp. Shattered the bulb everywhere on the carpet. It was one of those energy-saving bulbs, the curly ones? I read somewhere that they have mercury in them, and if you break one, you have to open all the windows and leave the room so you don't breathe in the toxic gas. Mercury is fascinating, though. Liquid metal. We used to actually play with it in middle school science class, just rolling it around on our hands and on the black lab tables. You could never do that today, the safety regulations are so strict. OSHA would have a field day. 

My brother-in-law used to work in compliance for a construction company, and all he did was complain about OSHA. He wore these massive steel-toed boots that clomped so loud on the hardwood floors. He scuffed up my hallway the last time he came over. I tried buffing it out with a tennis ball on a stick—I saw a janitor do that once at the high school—but it didn't really work. The scuff is still there, right by the baseboard. 

But... no. No one is putting thoughts in my head. It's just my own thoughts in there. Nothing like that.

It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are watching you with the intent to harm you, or do you think they’re just observing without any direct ill will?\n\nPatient: Harm me? I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. It's like... well, I know it sounds far-fetched, I keep saying that, but I'm just certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. I tried rolling up a towel and stuffing it under the door, but it didn't help much. \n\nAnyway... I don't know about ill will. I just know they're watching me. Waiting for me to slip up with the poisoned stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not entirely your own, or like something outside of you is putting ideas into your head?\n\nPatient: Thoughts not my own? No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. Nobody is putting ideas in my head. I mean, the only thing that gets put in my head are those stupid commercial jingles, you know? Earworms. There's this one for a local mattress store that just plays on a loop in my brain sometimes. \n\nI haven't bought a new mattress in... God, maybe fifteen years. It's an old coil spring one. They say you're supposed to flip them every six months, but it's so heavy. I tried flipping it by myself once and I knocked over my bedside lamp. Shattered the bulb everywhere on the carpet. It was one of those energy-saving bulbs, the curly ones? I read somewhere that they have mercury in them, and if you break one, you have to open all the windows and leave the room so you don't breathe in the toxic gas. Mercury is fascinating, though. Liquid metal. We used to actually play with it in middle school science class, just rolling it around on our hands and on the black lab tables. You could never do that today, the safety regulations are so strict. OSHA would have a field day. \n\nMy brother-in-law used to work in compliance for a construction company, and all he did was complain about OSHA. He wore these massive steel-toed boots that clomped so loud on the hardwood floors. He scuffed up my hallway the last time he came over. I tried buffing it out with a tennis ball on a stick—I saw a janitor do that once at the high school—but it didn't really work. The scuff is still there, right by the baseboard. \n\nBut... no. No one is putting thoughts in my head. It's just my own thoughts in there. Nothing like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are watching you with the intent to harm you, or do you think they’re just observing without any direct ill will?\n\nPatient: Harm me? I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. It's like... well, I know it sounds far-fetched, I keep saying that, but I'm just certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. I tried rolling up a towel and stuffing it under the door, but it didn't help much. \n\nAnyway... I don't know about ill will. I just know they're watching me. Waiting for me to slip up with the poisoned stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not entirely your own, or like something outside of you is putting ideas into your head?\n\nPatient: Thoughts not my own? No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. Nobody is putting ideas in my head. I mean, the only thing that gets put in my head are those stupid commercial jingles, you know? Earworms. There's this one for a local mattress store that just plays on a loop in my brain sometimes. \n\nI haven't bought a new mattress in... God, maybe fifteen years. It's an old coil spring one. They say you're supposed to flip them every six months, but it's so heavy. I tried flipping it by myself once and I knocked over my bedside lamp. Shattered the bulb everywhere on the carpet. It was one of those energy-saving bulbs, the curly ones? I read somewhere that they have mercury in them, and if you break one, you have to open all the windows and leave the room so you don't breathe in the toxic gas. Mercury is fascinating, though. Liquid metal. We used to actually play with it in middle school science class, just rolling it around on our hands and on the black lab tables. No one is putting thoughts in my head. It's just my own thoughts in there. Working together? I mean, I don't know if they have, like, a group chat or if they're holding meetings about me. But they're definitely all watching. Like I've been saying, and I know how far-fetched it sounds, I really do, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. They just all know about the poison, and they're all watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are watching you because of something specific you've done, or do you believe they're watching you for reasons you can't control?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's because of something I did. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are watching you with the intent to harm you, or do you think they’re just observing without any direct ill will?\n\nPatient: Harm me? And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. Waiting for me to slip up with the poisoned stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not entirely your own, or like something outside of you is putting ideas into your head?\n\nPatient: Thoughts not my own? No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. Nobody is putting ideas in my head. I mean, the only thing that gets put in my head are those stupid commercial jingles, you know? Earworms. It was one of those energy-saving bulbs, the curly ones? I read somewhere that they have mercury in them, and if you break one, you have to open all the windows and leave the room so you don't breathe in the toxic gas. Mercury is fascinating, though. Liquid metal. We used to actually play with it in middle school science class, just rolling it around on our hands and on the black lab tables. You could never do that today, the safety regulations are so strict. OSHA would have a field day. \n\nMy brother-in-law used to work in compliance for a construction company, and all he did was complain about OSHA. I tried buffing it out with a tennis ball on a stick—I saw a janitor do that once at the high school—but it didn't really work. The scuff is still there, right by the baseboard. \n\nBut... no. No one is putting thoughts in my head. It's just my own thoughts in there. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are all working together or sharing information about you, like they’re part of a coordinated effort to watch you?\n\nPatient: Working together? Man, RadioShack. I used to go there just to look at the remote-controlled cars. I saved up for months to buy this red dune buggy, but it took like eight AA batteries and they would die in fifteen minutes. Batteries are ridiculously expensive now. I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Acting on their own? I... I don't know if there's a grand plan or some mastermind pulling the strings. I just know what I know. Like I keep saying, and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every last thing. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again with all of it. 

Plans... you know, my ex-wife was a big planner. She had this color-coded calendar on the fridge, every kid's activity, every doctor's appointment, all mapped out. She used these dry-erase markers with the little erasers built into the caps. But the erasers always got smudged with ink after a while, and then they'd just smear black streaks all over the whiteboard. I tried cleaning it with rubbing alcohol once, but the smell gave me this massive headache. Rubbing alcohol has such a sharp, sterile smell. It reminds me of the nurse's office in elementary school. The nurse there, Mrs. Gable, she always kept a jar of peppermints on her desk. Those little round ones wrapped in clear cellophane with the red stripes. I used to go in there and pretend I had a stomachache just to get a mint. Peppermint is supposed to soothe your stomach, you know. I read an article in a magazine at the dentist's office about how peppermint oil can help with digestion. The dentist I went to had this huge collection of National Geographic magazines in the waiting room. The glossy pages always felt so nice to turn, but they were so heavy. You'd try to hold one up to read, and your wrists would start aching after ten minutes. I tried doing wrist curls with some light dumbbells to strengthen them, but I ended up tweaking my elbow...

So, I don't know if someone told them to watch me. I just know they are watching. Waiting for me to mess up with the poisoned stuff.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F23.80\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my wife made the appointment. She's been... she's been really worried lately. We've been married for twenty-two years, you know? Met at the community college back when I was getting my associate's degree. She was studying accounting, always so good with numbers. I was never good with numbers, always preferred working with my hands. But anyway, she's the one who wanted me to come here. What finally scared her is that I keep hearing things. Like, I hear water running, and these men whispering inside the walls. I tell her about it, but nobody's there when she checks. Our house is old, built in the fifties, got those plaster walls that are a nightmare to fix if you ever have to put a hole in them. I tried to hang a picture once and the whole section just crumbled. But it's not just the noise. Sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I'll go over and touch it, and it's completely dry. It doesn't make any sense. My wife's uncle had a plumbing leak that ruined his entire basement, mold everywhere, cost him thousands. I just... I don't know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything else that others might not notice, like shadows moving when no one is there or voices that aren't coming from any person nearby?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really shadows moving... but I do see what's actually happening. I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It just seeps into the furniture, the clothes, all of it. You know, they used to use this cloth insulation on wires back in the fifties, and it just degrades over time. My buddy Dave had to rewire his whole garage because of it a few years back. Cost him an arm and a leg, and the contractor left a mess of drywall dust everywhere. Drywall dust is terrible for your lungs, you really have to wear one of those heavy-duty masks if you're ever sanding it down. \n\nBut anyway... my things are all poisoned now. And the worst part is, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. They pretend they aren't, but they are. The guy next door, he's always out there messing with his sprinkler heads right when I happen to walk outside. Who adjusts their sprinklers every single day? It wastes so much water. The city actually sent out a notice last summer about water conservation, they wanted everyone to only water on even or odd days depending on your house number. I always followed the rules. But yeah... he's out there. Just waiting for me to make a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages in a way that others might not understand? Like things people say or do are directed at you specifically, even if they don't seem to be?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they're sending me secret messages or anything like that. It's more just... I know what's going on. I already mentioned it, but I know it sounds crazy, I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the old wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's just a fact. And the neighbors... they don't have to say anything to me. I can just tell they're watching me, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nLike that guy with the sprinklers. It's not just him, either. The lady across the street, she has this little yappy dog, a Pomeranian or something. My aunt had a Pomeranian when I was a kid, mean little thing, bit my ankle right through my sock once. Still have a tiny scar if you look close enough. Anyway, that lady is always walking that dog right past my driveway, real slow. But she never looks at me. That's how you know they're watching, right? When they go out of their way to pretend they aren't. They probably all talk about me at the neighborhood block party. We used to go to those, back when my wife would make her famous potato salad. She puts a little bit of dill in it, really changes the whole flavor profile. But I haven't had an appetite for potato salad, or anything really, in months. Everything just tastes like... dust. Poisoned dust.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people's actions — like the neighbor adjusting his sprinklers or the woman walking her dog — are specifically targeted at you or meant to harm or monitor you in some way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, exactly. Like I was just trying to explain... they are definitely watching me. I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do, but I am certain that the mold and the old wiring in the house have poisoned everything I own. It's ruined it all. And they know it. So they're monitoring me, just waiting for me to finally mess up. \n\nMonitoring... you know, speaking of monitoring, my brother-in-law put up one of those doorbell cameras last Thanksgiving. The ones that send a video right to your phone? He caught a raccoon trying to get into his garbage cans at two in the morning. Raccoons are incredibly smart, they have those little hands that can open latches. We had one get into our camping cooler back in '98, ate all the hot dogs but left the buns. My wife was so mad, she had spent all morning packing that cooler with ice. We had to drive twenty miles to a gas station just to get something for dinner... \n\nBut... what was I saying? Right. The neighbors. They don't need doorbell cameras to monitor me. They just use their sprinklers and their dogs. They're absolutely targeting me, just watching and waiting for the poison to finally take me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are trying to harm you or that they are plotting against you in any way beyond just watching you?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Well, they don't really have to plot anything, because the harm is already done. I know I sound like a broken record here, and I know how far-fetched it all sounds, but I am just certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have already poisoned everything I own. The damage is done. So the neighbors don't need to do anything to me directly. They're just watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, they actually used to have a formal Neighborhood Watch program on our street. They put up these reflective metal signs on the light posts. The sign had a little cartoon burglar on it, wearing a domino mask and a striped shirt. I always thought that was so stupid. Who wears a black and white striped shirt to sneak around? That's terrible camouflage. If you really want to blend into the shadows, you shouldn't even wear pure black, you should wear dark navy blue. I read that in a hunting magazine in a dentist's waiting room once. My uncle used to go bow hunting up in Wisconsin every fall. He had this old A-frame cabin, but it didn't have indoor plumbing, so you had to use an outhouse. Let me tell you, walking out there in the pitch dark in November... freezing. Absolutely freezing. You couldn't feel your toes for an hour after you got back in your sleeping bag. \n\nBut... yeah. No, the neighbors aren't plotting to hurt me. The poison from the house is already doing that. They're just waiting and watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that something or someone is controlling or influencing the neighbors or the environment in your house, like a hidden force or power that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: A hidden force? No, no, it’s nothing like that. No mind control or government rays or whatever. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, but I am just absolutely certain it’s the mold and the old wiring in that house. That's what has poisoned everything I own. And the neighbors... nobody is forcing them to do anything, they’re just watching on their own to see when I mess up again. \n\nWiring, though... you know, I actually took a couple of electrical classes back when I was getting my associate's degree. I thought about doing that for a living, being an electrician. My buddy Dave actually did it, and he makes really good money. He bought this massive truck a few years ago, a Ford F-250 with the dual rear wheels. We took it down to the lake once to go fishing for bass, but he backed it too far down the boat ramp and got completely stuck in the mud. We had to sit there for three hours waiting for a tow. The tow truck driver was eating a meatball sub the whole time he was hooking up the winch. Just dripping marinara sauce right onto his leather work boots. I remember thinking, how do you even eat something that messy when you're working? I couldn't do it. Honestly, I haven't even had a meatball in... I don't know. I don't really eat much of anything anymore anyway. Meatballs just sound way too heavy. Everything sits in my stomach like lead. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what was I saying? Right, hidden powers. No. No hidden powers. Just the poison from the house, and the neighbors watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like other people can tell your belongings are poisoned, or do you think it's something only you can sense?\n\nPatient: They can tell. I mean, they have to be able to tell, right? That's why they're watching me. Like I keep saying—and I know, I know it sounds completely far-fetched, but I am just certain of it—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And the neighbors know it. They can tell, and they're just watching to see when I finally mess up again. \n\nMess up... you know, speaking of messing up, I tried to fix a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom last spring. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip. Drove me absolutely crazy at night. I went to the hardware store and bought all the washers and a new wrench. The guy at the hardware store was wearing this bright orange apron. It reminded me of those safety vests they make highway workers wear. I always feel bad for those guys, working out in the blazing sun all summer with the asphalt just radiating heat. I remember driving through Nevada once, years ago, and the road looked like it was melting. We had to pull over at this little diner because the car was overheating. I ordered a slice of cherry pie, but the cherries were so tart I couldn't even finish it. My wife ended up eating it. She always loved sour things. Sour Patch Kids, lemon drops... she used to buy those giant bags of lemons and just eat them like oranges. I never understood how she didn't ruin the enamel on her teeth. The dentist always told her to be careful with that. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, if other people can sense the poison. Yeah, the neighbors definitely know. That's why they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that other people, like friends or family members, also know that your belongings are poisoned, or do you think you're the only one who truly understands it?\n\nPatient: They know. My family, I mean. I haven't seen them much lately, but they know. They have to. Like I've been saying, and I know how completely crazy and far-fetched it sounds, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, just like the neighbors know it. They're all just watching to see when I mess up again.\n\nFamily... my sister actually called me a couple of weeks ago. Or maybe it was a month ago? I can't keep track of time anymore. She lives out in Oregon now. Portland. It rains all the time out there. I went to visit her once, maybe ten years ago, and I forgot to pack an umbrella. I had to buy one at this little corner store near her apartment. It was one of those cheap, flimsy ones with a plastic handle shaped like a duck's head. I thought it was funny at first, but the beak kept catching on my coat sleeve. It was a nice coat, too—a gray wool peacoat I found at a thrift store for like twenty bucks. You don't find deals like that anymore. Thrift stores used to be great, but now everybody goes to them and they jack up the prices. I saw a used toaster at a Goodwill once for thirty dollars. Thirty dollars! You can buy a brand new one at Target for twenty. And you don't even know if the used one works. Or what kind of bread they put in it. Some people eat that really dense pumpernickel bread. It always smells a little bit like dirt to me. I used to try to eat it when I was trying to be healthy, but... well, like I said, I don't really have an appetite for anything these days anyway. \n\nBut... yeah. My family knows. They know about the poison, and they know the neighbors are watching me. Everyone knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family or the neighbors are working together in any way regarding the poisoning, or do you believe they each just know about it independently?\n\nPatient: Working together? I... I don't know if they talk to each other about it. But they both know. Like I keep telling you—and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am just absolutely certain—the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. My family knows it, and I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nWatching... you know, my neighbor across the street, he has this massive German Shepherd. Beautiful dog, but it just sits in the front window all day long, staring out. Just watching the street. It barks at the mailman every single day, right at 11:30. You could set your watch by it. I used to have a watch, a nice Seiko with a leather band. My grandfather gave it to me when I graduated high school. It had this little dial that showed the phases of the moon. I always thought that was so fascinating, tracking the moon. I wanted to be an astronaut when I was a kid. Didn't everyone? I had this poster of the Apollo 11 moon landing on my bedroom wall, right next to a poster of a Lamborghini Countach. The white one. Man, those cars looked like spaceships themselves. My cousin actually got to sit in one once at a car show in Chicago. Chicago gets so cold in the winter, the wind just cuts right through you off the lake. I went there for a conference once, and I forgot my scarf. I had to buy this really itchy wool one at a kiosk in the train station. It made my neck break out in a rash. \n\nBut... I'm sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, if they work together. I don't think they're coordinating or anything. They just all know about the poison, and they're all watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are watching you because of something specific you've done, or do you believe they're watching you for reasons you can't control?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's because of something I did. I just... I know it sounds far-fetched, I really do, but I am absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... I used to worry about messing up all the time when I worked at the printing press. That was years ago, back before everything went digital. We had these massive Heidelberg presses, huge metal machines that were so loud you had to wear earplugs all day or you'd go deaf. The smell of the ink was incredible, though. It was this sharp, chemical smell, but sweet at the same time. I loved that smell. I used to come home and my hands would be stained blue or magenta for days, no matter how hard I scrubbed with that gritty pumice soap. Pumice is volcanic rock, you know. It's crazy to think about washing your hands with a volcano. I saw a documentary about Mount St. Helens once. The ash cloud was so huge it blocked out the sun for hundreds of miles. People had to wear masks just to breathe, kind of like when we had all those wildfires a few years back and the sky turned that eerie orange color. The ash was falling like snow on my car. It ruined the clear coat on the hood. I tried buffing it out with this special wax I bought online, but it just made it look cloudy. \n\nBut... I don't think I can control it. The neighbors watching, I mean. They just know about the poison, and they're waiting for me to slip up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are watching you with the intent to harm you, or do you think they’re just observing without any direct ill will?\n\nPatient: Harm me? I... I don't know if they want to hurt me, exactly. But they're not just observing, either. It's like... well, I know it sounds far-fetched, I keep saying that, but I'm just certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. I tried rolling up a towel and stuffing it under the door, but it didn't help much. \n\nAnyway... I don't know about ill will. I just know they're watching me. Waiting for me to slip up with the poisoned stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not entirely your own, or like something outside of you is putting ideas into your head?\n\nPatient: Thoughts not my own? No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. Nobody is putting ideas in my head. I mean, the only thing that gets put in my head are those stupid commercial jingles, you know? Earworms. There's this one for a local mattress store that just plays on a loop in my brain sometimes. \n\nI haven't bought a new mattress in... God, maybe fifteen years. It's an old coil spring one. They say you're supposed to flip them every six months, but it's so heavy. I tried flipping it by myself once and I knocked over my bedside lamp. Shattered the bulb everywhere on the carpet. It was one of those energy-saving bulbs, the curly ones? I read somewhere that they have mercury in them, and if you break one, you have to open all the windows and leave the room so you don't breathe in the toxic gas. Mercury is fascinating, though. Liquid metal. We used to actually play with it in middle school science class, just rolling it around on our hands and on the black lab tables. You could never do that today, the safety regulations are so strict. OSHA would have a field day. \n\nMy brother-in-law used to work in compliance for a construction company, and all he did was complain about OSHA. He wore these massive steel-toed boots that clomped so loud on the hardwood floors. He scuffed up my hallway the last time he came over. I tried buffing it out with a tennis ball on a stick—I saw a janitor do that once at the high school—but it didn't really work. The scuff is still there, right by the baseboard. \n\nBut... no. No one is putting thoughts in my head. It's just my own thoughts in there. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are all working together or sharing information about you, like they’re part of a coordinated effort to watch you?\n\nPatient: Working together? I mean, I don't know if they have, like, a group chat or if they're holding meetings about me. But they're definitely all watching. Like I've been saying, and I know how far-fetched it sounds, I really do, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And I can just tell they're all watching to see when I mess up again with the poisoned stuff. \n\nCoordinated... it makes me think of those neighborhood watch programs. They used to hand out those little window decals, the ones with the eyeball on them? My dad was obsessed with those. He bought this heavy-duty set of walkie-talkies from RadioShack back in the day. Man, RadioShack. I used to go there just to look at the remote-controlled cars. I saved up for months to buy this red dune buggy, but it took like eight AA batteries and they would die in fifteen minutes. Batteries are ridiculously expensive now. I tried buying that huge pack they sell at Costco, but I just ended up losing it in my junk drawer. My kitchen drawers are a complete disaster right now. I bought those little plastic divider trays to organize everything, but the big spoons and spatulas always get wedged in the back, so the drawer jams when you try to pull it open. It's so frustrating. I had this really great spatula with a wooden handle, perfect for pancakes, but the wood cracked down the middle because I accidentally ran it through the dishwasher. You're never supposed to put wood in the dishwasher, the heat totally ruins it. My grandmother had this massive wooden salad bowl that she had to hand-wash and rub with mineral oil every month... \n\nSo, I don't know if they're sharing information or what. I just know they're watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are acting on their own when they watch you, or do you think someone told them to watch you or that they're following some kind of plan?\n\nPatient: Acting on their own? I... I don't know if there's a grand plan or some mastermind pulling the strings. I just know what I know. Like I keep saying, and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every last thing. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again with all of it. \n\nPlans... you know, my ex-wife was a big planner. She had this color-coded calendar on the fridge, every kid's activity, every doctor's appointment, all mapped out. She used these dry-erase markers with the little erasers built into the caps. But the erasers always got smudged with ink after a while, and then they'd just smear black streaks all over the whiteboard. I tried cleaning it with rubbing alcohol once, but the smell gave me this massive headache. Rubbing alcohol has such a sharp, sterile smell. It reminds me of the nurse's office in elementary school. The nurse there, Mrs. Gable, she always kept a jar of peppermints on her desk. Those little round ones wrapped in clear cellophane with the red stripes. I used to go in there and pretend I had a stomachache just to get a mint. Peppermint is supposed to soothe your stomach, you know. I read an article in a magazine at the dentist's office about how peppermint oil can help with digestion. The dentist I went to had this huge collection of National Geographic magazines in the waiting room. The glossy pages always felt so nice to turn, but they were so heavy. You'd try to hold one up to read, and your wrists would start aching after ten minutes. I tried doing wrist curls with some light dumbbells to strengthen them, but I ended up tweaking my elbow...\n\nSo, I don't know if someone told them to watch me. I just know they are watching. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. \n\nMess up... it's like they're waiting for a show. Like when you go to the movies and you're just waiting for the main feature to start. I haven't been to a movie theater in years. The floors are always so sticky. I don't know what they spill on them, but it feels like your shoes are going to get glued to the carpet. I had a pair of sneakers once, these really comfortable New Balance ones, and I stepped in a patch of wet cement outside the grocery store. The construction workers hadn't put up any cones or caution tape or anything. It was just this gray square of doom right on the sidewalk. I tried to scrape it off with a stick, but it dried into the treads and threw off my whole stride. I started getting this terrible pain in my lower back because I was walking funny. I went to a chiropractor for it, this guy who had a fish tank in his waiting room. It was huge, took up a whole wall, and he had these neon tetras that would school together and dart around. They were so bright, almost like they were glowing. I asked him what he fed them, but he just cracked my neck and told me to put ice on my back. Ice is the worst. It just makes you cold. I hate being cold. My old apartment had the worst draft by the front door. You could literally feel the wind blowing in if you stood there. I tried rolling up a towel and stuffing it under the door, but it didn't help much. \n\nAnyway... I don't know about ill will. I just know they're watching me. Waiting for me to slip up with the poisoned stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not entirely your own, or like something outside of you is putting ideas into your head?\n\nPatient: Thoughts not my own? No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. Nobody is putting ideas in my head. I mean, the only thing that gets put in my head are those stupid commercial jingles, you know? Earworms. There's this one for a local mattress store that just plays on a loop in my brain sometimes. \n\nI haven't bought a new mattress in... God, maybe fifteen years. It's an old coil spring one. They say you're supposed to flip them every six months, but it's so heavy. I tried flipping it by myself once and I knocked over my bedside lamp. Shattered the bulb everywhere on the carpet. It was one of those energy-saving bulbs, the curly ones? I read somewhere that they have mercury in them, and if you break one, you have to open all the windows and leave the room so you don't breathe in the toxic gas. Mercury is fascinating, though. Liquid metal. We used to actually play with it in middle school science class, just rolling it around on our hands and on the black lab tables. You could never do that today, the safety regulations are so strict. OSHA would have a field day. \n\nMy brother-in-law used to work in compliance for a construction company, and all he did was complain about OSHA. He wore these massive steel-toed boots that clomped so loud on the hardwood floors. He scuffed up my hallway the last time he came over. I tried buffing it out with a tennis ball on a stick—I saw a janitor do that once at the high school—but it didn't really work. The scuff is still there, right by the baseboard. \n\nBut... no. No one is putting thoughts in my head. It's just my own thoughts in there. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are all working together or sharing information about you, like they’re part of a coordinated effort to watch you?\n\nPatient: Working together? I mean, I don't know if they have, like, a group chat or if they're holding meetings about me. But they're definitely all watching. Like I've been saying, and I know how far-fetched it sounds, I really do, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Every single thing. And I can just tell they're all watching to see when I mess up again with the poisoned stuff. \n\nCoordinated... it makes me think of those neighborhood watch programs. They used to hand out those little window decals, the ones with the eyeball on them? My dad was obsessed with those. He bought this heavy-duty set of walkie-talkies from RadioShack back in the day. Man, RadioShack. I used to go there just to look at the remote-controlled cars. I saved up for months to buy this red dune buggy, but it took like eight AA batteries and they would die in fifteen minutes. Batteries are ridiculously expensive now. I tried buying that huge pack they sell at Costco, but I just ended up losing it in my junk drawer. My kitchen drawers are a complete disaster right now. I bought those little plastic divider trays to organize everything, but the big spoons and spatulas always get wedged in the back, so the drawer jams when you try to pull it open. It's so frustrating. I had this really great spatula with a wooden handle, perfect for pancakes, but the wood cracked down the middle because I accidentally ran it through the dishwasher. You're never supposed to put wood in the dishwasher, the heat totally ruins it. My grandmother had this massive wooden salad bowl that she had to hand-wash and rub with mineral oil every month... \n\nSo, I don't know if they're sharing information or what. I just know they're watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the neighbors are acting on their own when they watch you, or do you think someone told them to watch you or that they're following some kind of plan?\n\nPatient: Acting on their own? I... I don't know if there's a grand plan or some mastermind pulling the strings. I just know what I know. Like I keep saying, and I know, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every last thing. And I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again with all of it. \n\nPlans... you know, my ex-wife was a big planner. She had this color-coded calendar on the fridge, every kid's activity, every doctor's appointment, all mapped out. She used these dry-erase markers with the little erasers built into the caps. But the erasers always got smudged with ink after a while, and then they'd just smear black streaks all over the whiteboard. I tried cleaning it with rubbing alcohol once, but the smell gave me this massive headache. Rubbing alcohol has such a sharp, sterile smell. It reminds me of the nurse's office in elementary school. The nurse there, Mrs. Gable, she always kept a jar of peppermints on her desk. Those little round ones wrapped in clear cellophane with the red stripes. I used to go in there and pretend I had a stomachache just to get a mint. Peppermint is supposed to soothe your stomach, you know. I read an article in a magazine at the dentist's office about how peppermint oil can help with digestion. You'd try to hold one up to read, and your wrists would start aching after ten minutes. I tried doing wrist curls with some light dumbbells to strengthen them, but I ended up tweaking my elbow...\n\nSo, I don't know if someone told them to watch me. I just know they are watching. Working together? I... I don't know if they have some kind of schedule, like they take shifts or something. I just know what I know. Like I've been telling you, and I know it sounds crazy, I really do, but I am just absolutely certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Every single thing I have. And I can tell they're watching me to see when I mess up again with all the poisoned stuff. 

Schedules, though... my buddy Dave used to work the graveyard shift at a bottling plant, and his schedule was completely backwards. He'd sleep all day and be up all night. He had to put tin foil over his bedroom windows just to keep the sun out so he could get some rest. Tin foil is amazing, isn't it? You can use it for so many things. I tried to use it once to boost the signal on my WiFi router, shaped it like a little satellite dish behind the antennas. I saw a video about it online. The internet is full of those little life hacks. Most of them don't work, though. Like the one where you put a wooden spoon over a boiling pot of pasta to keep it from boiling over? Completely useless. The water just bubbled right over the spoon and made a huge starchy mess on my stovetop. It took me an hour to scrub it off. I had to use this special heavy-duty degreaser I bought at the hardware store. The hardware store down the street from me has this old wooden floor that creaks every time you take a step. It smells like sawdust and fertilizer in there. I love that smell. It reminds me of my grandfather's garage...

But, yeah, I don't know if they're coordinating it all. I just know they are watching me. Waiting for me to mess up.
